| LWB-R359 | Manage Overview composite receipt and recheck ownership | Ready for Review | Manage Overview was server-composed from Portfolio 360 plus five Manage reads, but the browser had no governed composite owner, visible receipt, or bounded recheck. Reload was the only recovery path, and a future client cache could have retained incomplete evidence without an exact admission invariant. | Under #1050, introduced one TanStack Query identity containing portfolio, session, date, period, and currency; forwarded cancellation through all six reads; disabled mount, focus, reconnect, and remount reads; and added one deliberate **Recheck overview** action. Only a complete exact-context composite advances **Checked**. Ordinary failure retains the last admission, permission refusal withholds it, and delayed prior-context results cannot replace the active view. Isolated the client boundary to Overview, kept the other eight Manage modes server-composed, and consolidated status, receipt, and action into the existing page header instead of adding another card. | Complete full gates, protected PR review/CI, exact-main validation, wiki publication/parity, issue closure, and branch cleanup. Keep browser receipt time distinct from source business date or certified freshness; do not add polling or another cache abstraction. | Workbench #1050/#787; focused loader, Query, component, failure, permission, remount, reconnect, focus, and delayed-context tests; browser proof of exactly one request to each required path and page height below the existing 1,200px gate. |
| LWB-R358 | Adviser Book receipt and governed recheck ownership | Ready for Review | Adviser Book had strict source admission but kept response, loading, error, request sequencing, and retry state in a component hook. Returning to an unchanged register could repay a read, while the adviser had no receipt age or bounded way to deliberately recheck the displayed assignment evidence. | Under #1048, moved the exact register identity to the existing root Query owner, propagated cancellation, disabled automatic mount/focus/reconnect reads, deleted the parallel sequencing machinery, and added one quiet **Checked** receipt plus **Recheck book** action beside the result scope. Ordinary recheck failure retains the prior admitted register and receipt with explicit copy; permission refusal removes it. | Complete protected PR gates, exact-main validation, wiki publication/parity, issue closure, and branch hygiene. Do not turn browser receipt time into source freshness or add polling. | Workbench #1048/#787; exact request-count browser proof; focused API, Query, component, failure, permission, remount, reconnect, focus, and late-context tests. |
| LWB-R357 | Verified BFF principal and client-state isolation | In Progress | Promoted Workbench routes failed closed but had no executable signed-session verifier, current-grant resolution, delegated Gateway credential, or complete client-state boundary for an authority transition. Browser aliases were stripped, yet no positive source path could attribute an admitted banker. Query clearing alone would also have retained feature-owned responses and receipts after a principal change. | Under #436, implemented Ed25519 issuer/audience/time/revocation verification, thirteen-class fail-closed principal/grant resolution, user/application intersection, short-lived delegated credentials, and exact route/scope admission for Idea, Adviser Book, Adviser Cockpit, Advisory Copilot, and Report Centre. Verified posture forwards no authority headers. Responses expose only an opaque authority digest; a transition clears Query state, remounts protected feature state, and rejects late prior-authority responses. Authenticated denial carries an explicit clear marker so logout, expiry, revocation, or unavailable grants cannot leave prior-principal data rendered. Route rejection and requirement policy moved out of the 432-line route, reducing it to 282 lines. | Keep configured production admission closed until the security/identity owner provides the tenant-membership and grant resolver. Do not infer IAM grants from Core portfolio-party relationships. Obtain Gateway delegated-credential enforcement, live IdP and key-custody evidence, protected browser denial/admission proof, exact-main validation, and deployment certification before closing #436. | Workbench #436; Platform principal-credential and principal-resolution contracts; real Ed25519 positive/foreign-key tests; all denial, zero-protected-call, exact capability/scope, hostile-header, delegated scope-lookup, Query-clear, feature-remount, authenticated-denial, and late-response tests. |
| LWB-R356 | Risk attribution source-state presentation | Hardened | Gateway publishes `ready`, `partial`, `unavailable`, or `blocked` attribution evidence, but the view model handled only blocked and unavailable before falling through to ready. Partial proxy and mixed-failure results therefore rendered full magnitude tracks beside qualified evidence; an unfamiliar runtime value could take the same path. | Under #1032, made every presentation state explicit, retained supported contributor facts and controls for partial evidence, withheld magnitude tracks outside exact ready, added a compact business qualification, and failed unfamiliar states through the unavailable path. Query admission and recovery tests preserve Gateway state without inventing raw Risk vocabulary. | Keep the visual gate state-based. Do not infer readiness from one reason code, a zero degraded count, or equality between contribution share and weight. Risk #291 / Performance #514 own any future per-group return-series capability. | Workbench #1032; focused query/recovery, view-model and component tests; governed populated Risk browser proof for ready and mixed partial evidence; rendered evidence at `output/playwright/risk-attribution-partial-evidence.png`. |
| LWB-R355 | Advisor Commentary ordering authority | Hardened | Report Centre exposed `advisor_brief_run_id` as free text and loaded a context-free catalogue, even though Gateway publishes accepted-brief availability for an exact report date and currency. An adviser could not distinguish review-required, context-mismatch, or source-unknown posture, and a changed context could retain an older commentary choice. | Under #990, admitted the exact Gateway availability contract, requested it with current date/currency, removed manual run-id entry, projected distinct business states and recovery, submitted only the source-returned accepted run, disabled commentary for portfolio bundles, cleared prior bindings on context change, and fenced late or failed refreshes. | Complete optimized browser proof, protected exact-head CI/review, rebase merge, exact-main validation, wiki publication/parity, and issue evidence. Keep completed-report section outcomes and per-claim grounding open until Gateway publishes a supported consumer surface. | Workbench #990/#795; W3C form/error guidance and BlackRock adviser-commentary workflow research; focused contract, API, view-model, component, race, and browser tests. |
| LWB-R354 | Exact transaction hydration and response classification | Hardened | The URL-addressed exact-record query disabled retries but still inherited automatic focus, reconnect, and remount behavior, including retry-on-mount after a failed read. Its state could also be collected after the default five-minute inactive window, allowing a later remount to pay another Gateway read without adviser intent. A successful response with an empty or malformed JSON body bypassed the typed contract failure and appeared as generic unavailability. | Under #1017, the exact query now suppresses retry-on-mount, window-focus, reconnect, and remount reads and retains its exact result for the QueryClient session, while keeping one visible source retry for the unchanged active query identity. Decode failures flow through the existing strict transaction parser and become `source_contract_invalid`; query-key ownership continues to fence late results by portfolio, transaction, as-of date, currency, and projected posture. | Complete protected PR checks, exact-main releasability, wiki publication/parity, QA closure, and branch hygiene. Keep #1014 report receipt admission unchanged. | Workbench #1017 and merged PR #1015; focused API/component proof for successful and failed remount beyond the normal collection window, window focus, reconnect, explicit retry, empty/malformed 2xx bodies, and prior-context race fencing. |
| LWB-R353 | Single-report acceptance receipt binding | Hardened | Report Centre validated a batch acceptance against its reviewed key and portfolio count, but stored every structurally valid single-report 2xx handle and immediately presented acceptance. A response for another reviewed intent or a status link naming another job could therefore become the visible support record. | Under #1004, added one pure single-report receipt-admission boundary before handle storage or accepted state. It requires the exact reviewed idempotency key and the Gateway-published status-path prefix with one encoded segment that decodes to the returned opaque job id. Malformed or mismatched receipts retain the reviewed setup as not accepted; unchanged retries reuse the exact request and key; valid acceptance remains distinct from a failed subsequent history refresh. The existing batch safeguards and workspace-generation fences are unchanged. | Complete protected exact-head review/CI, rebase merge, exact-main validation, wiki publication/parity, #1004 closure, and branch hygiene. Do not broaden this consumer check into Report-owned job policy or infer success from HTTP status. | Workbench #1004; Gateway main `4eecdd3b`; 68 focused contract/admission assertions, 63 rendered-workspace assertions, and optimized browser proof that a mismatched status reference never publishes success or a support handle. |

| LWB-R350 | Shared BFF response-byte and header authority | Hardened | The BFF buffered decoded Fetch bodies but forwarded upstream `Content-Encoding` and `Content-Length`, constructed nominal bodies for every status, and removed only `Transfer-Encoding`. Consider streaming only with measured need and explicit timeout, cancellation, range, and backpressure ownership. No screen-guide, screenshot, dependency, Gateway, or platform-skill change is required. | Workbench #1003; real route regressions for decoded JSON, identity PDF, `HEAD`, `204`, `205`, `304`, and `206`; request-boundary and syntax-aware BFF governance suites. |

Sequence traversal follows only the value-producing right operand, and simple assignment expressions contribute their right-hand value. A shared write-order comparator applies contained RHS writes before their enclosing assignment across binding, property, and authority replay. Logical assignments snapshot their pre-RHS authority so nested writes run only when `&&=`, `||=`, or `??=` reaches the RHS; uncertain execution preserves both outcomes. Statically resolved const and aliased-const keys share the same property lookup. Binding resolution follows repository alias paths through property, computed-property, renamed-destructured, member, and assignment-form intermediate values. Cyclic candidate traversal keys visits by symbol and program point, so a source may be safely revisited at an earlier capture while a repeated identical state becomes conservative unknown. Repository-program analysis now carries ordered property state through direct, bracket, dynamic-key, nested, namespace and ordered local-alias mutation; executable namespace, direct, computed-key, const-aliased, transitively aliased, named-destructured, assignment-form, mutable-initializer, mutable-destructured, logical-assignment, conditional/logical-RHS, tuple-initialized, statically aliased, or defaulted-destructuring Object/Reflect APIs, bounded conditional/logical destructuring owners, nested tuple-assignment patterns, and const-object member API aliases and calls, including getter-backed members with opaque-call barriers; statically inspectable `Object.assign` sources, spreads, descriptor objects, and descriptor maps; dynamic computed values; property-specific removal and numeric-update state; descriptor enumerability, including const-alias chains and signed BigInt flags; and index-specific collection transforms. A reusable proposal-money boundary allows at most two decimal places, validates exact scaled minor units, caps conversion below 2^46 major units where adjacent cents remain distinguishable, retains source/draft monetary arithmetic as integer minor units through the complete projection, and owns cash-movement field recovery, net display, preview arithmetic, and submitted decimal strings. | LWB-R221 | Render-pure outcome-review handoff context fencing | Hardened | `use-outcome-review-handoffs.ts` mirrored the selected source-review identity into a ref by assigning `.current` during render. The React Compiler evaluator rejected the write, and render-time mutation was incompatible with React's restartable render assumptions. Replacing it with sequence checks alone would have weakened the existing report safety rule because a source switch during report-input retrieval must prevent the subsequent report mutation, while a switch during either report or AI completion must prevent stale status, material, and communication-boundary publication. | Moved source-context synchronization to a committed layout effect and invalidated both report and AI request sequences only when the committed source identity changes. Both workflows require the captured request sequence and exact committed source-context key to remain current before a dependent mutation, after completion, and before error/result publication. Pending posture carries the exact context and request sequence; the committed context-change effect retires only the exact prior token before paint, and a superseded completion can clear only its own request.
